| 2026-07-01 | **Speaker verify: embed per-utterance PCM span (was rolling-ring tail).** `BaseSession` now accumulates `_utterance_pcm` (new `bytearray`) in `process_audio_frame`: on the first forwarded chunk of each new utterance (`_stt_audio_start == 0.0`) the buffer is cleared and restarts; subsequent chunks extend it, capped to `speaker_embed_window_sec`. `_verify_speaker` snapshots `bytes(self._utterance_pcm)` before the `asyncio.to_thread` embed call instead of slicing `_audio_ring`. Removed `_utterance_window_bytes()` (now unused). Zero overhead when `_speaker_verifier is None`. Branch `feat/seperate-service`. | Rolling-ring tail could include trailing silence, agent echo, or the previous speaker's tail, weakening enroll + verify accuracy especially on short utterances. |
| 2026-06-30 | **STT input gate + speaker verification.** New `voice/stt_input_gate.py::SttInputGate` (`STT_INPUT_GATE_MODE=off/rms/vad`): blocks PCM from the STT engine in noisy conditions. `rms` mode replaces the removed `AUDIO_GUARD_FRAMES_GATE_ENABLED`; `vad` reuses Silero VAD (already driving interrupts); all modes include a `AUDIO_GATE_PREROLL_CHUNKS` lookback so speech onset is not clipped. Wired in `base_session.process_audio_frame` (VAD runs first, then gate). New `voice/speaker_verifier.py::SpeakerVerifier + SpeakerEmbedder`: enroll-once on first valid utterance, cosine-match on subsequent (`SPEAKER_MATCH_THRESHOLD`), online adapt (`SPEAKER_ADAPT_ENABLED`). Embedder is remote-default (`SPEAKER_REMOTE_URL` → `noble-speaker-service` HTTP `/embed`) or local sherpa CAM++ ONNX (HF auto-provisioned). Runs at `_on_final` off the event loop via `asyncio.to_thread`. `voice/service/speaker_server.py` + `noble-speaker-service` console script. `prewarm_voice_models()` gates on `enabled AND not remote`. Branch `feat/seperate-service`. | Noise → garbage STT text degraded turn quality; background speakers polluted Luna's context. Both issues now have opt-in guards configurable purely via env. |
| 2026-06-30 | **DashScope/Qwen wired as a distinct LLM provider — three OpenAI-compat slots no longer share `OPENAI_*`.** `facade.build_llm_providers` now builds a `dashscope` slot (`openai.AsyncOpenAI(api_key=DASHSCOPE_API_KEY, base_url=DASHSCOPE_BASE_URL)`, gated on `DASHSCOPE_API_KEY`), inserted after `openai` → default chain `anthropic → openai → dashscope → gemini` (vLLM ON → `vllm → openai → dashscope → gemini → anthropic`). `config.provider_model_map` adds `"dashscope": DASHSCOPE_MODEL` so `primary_for` + summary/semcache paths resolve it; `thinking.disable_thinking_kwargs("dashscope")` returns `{"extra_body":{"enable_thinking":False}}` (DashScope's own top-level switch, NOT vLLM's `chat_template_kwargs`). The `DASHSCOPE_*`/`VLLM_*`/`OPENAI_*` config fields already existed — DashScope was just never constructed (dead config). `.env`/`.env.example` split into three independent slots: `qwen`→`DASHSCOPE_*` (primary via `NOBLE_AGENT_MODEL=qwen3.6-flash`), `local`→`VLLM_*` (opt-in via `VLLM_MODEL`), `openai`→`OPENAI_*` (hosted, default `api.openai.com`). Also fixed an inline-comment trap: `VLLM_MODEL=  # ...` made python-dotenv parse the comment AS the value → vLLM falsely enabled; comments moved to their own lines. Verified live: chain `anthropic → dashscope → gemini`, primary=dashscope. Branch `feat/seperate-service`. | User overloaded the single `OPENAI_*` slot for local vLLM + DashScope/Qwen + real OpenAI, so switching to the real OpenAI API kept hitting the wrong `OPENAI_BASE_URL`. Each backend now has its own slot; switch by editing env, no source change. (`OPENROUTER_*`/`GROQ_*`/`CEREBRAS_*` remain defined-but-unwired.) |
| 2026-06-30 | **Remote embed service: Bearer auth + `is_query` protocol.** The hosted e5-large embed service (microservice deploy) requires `Authorization: Bearer` and uses body `{"texts":[...],"is_query":bool}` (response `{"embeddings":[[...]]}`). `_Embedder` (`agent/rag/flat_store.py`) now reads `EMBED_REMOTE_TOKEN` (env-first, then new `config.embed_remote_token`) and attaches the Bearer header to its `httpx.Client` (empty token → no header, back-compat with token-less sidecars); `_encode_remote(texts, is_query)` posts `is_query` instead of the legacy `normalize` (`encode_query`→`True`, `encode_batch`→`False`). No behavior change when `EMBED_REMOTE_URL` is unset (in-process fastembed). Pure-config otherwise: MongoDB/Redis/Qdrant were already env-driven — `.env` now points at the hosted stack (`MONGODB_URI`/`MONGODB_DATABASE`, `REDIS_URL`, `QDRANT_URL`/`QDRANT_API_KEY`, `EMBED_REMOTE_URL`/`_TOKEN`) with `NOBLE_RAG_BACKEND=qdrant`, so the worker loads NO local embed model or `.npy` (remote embed + remote Qdrant dense + local BM25). Verified live end-to-end against all four services. Branch `feat/seperate-service`. | System moved to microservices: MongoDB/Redis/Qdrant/e5-large embed are now standalone hosted services; the embed service is Bearer-authed (the old sidecar protocol sent no header → 401). |
| 2026-06-30 | **TTS playback smoothness: output cushion + clause prefetch.** A turn is split by `SentenceBuffer` into many clauses, each a separate synth with its own ~300 ms TTFB; the old `tts_consumer` synthesized them strictly sequentially → a ~300 ms underrun at every clause seam → choppy. Two fixes: **(1) cushion** — `NOBLE_TTS_OUTPUT_QUEUE_MS` (`tts_output_queue_ms`, default **800**, was hard-coded 40 in `agent_audio_io.DEFAULT_QUEUE_SIZE_MS`) wired into `transport/livekit/worker.py` `AudioIOConfig(queue_size_ms=...)`; the LiveKit `AudioSource` playout buffer now bridges the seam gap. Safe for barge-in: `base_session._handle_interrupt` calls `audio_output.clear()` → `AudioSource.clear_queue()`, dropping the buffer instantly; does NOT delay first audio (`capture_frame` returns while there's room). **(2) prefetch** — `voice/voice_agent.py` `tts_consumer` split into `_synth_ahead()` (synthesizes clause N+1 into a per-clause `frame_q` while N plays; look-ahead depth 1 via `ready_queue=Queue(maxsize=1)` → ≤2 clauses buffered) + `_pacer()` (sends frames in order, realtime-paced by `capture_frame` backpressure). Barge-in is deadlock-free: pacer `_drain_and_cancel_synth()` cancels the synth task + drains `ready_queue` to unblock a maxsize=1 `put`; `contextlib.aclosing` on both generator iterations runs the remote-synth stream's `finally` (closes the WS) promptly instead of leaking to GC. Invariants preserved: in-order playback, `agent_speaking`/`sc.id`, `_last_completed_id` only on clean completion, both `immediate`/`finish_current` interrupt modes, `agent_done`. Metrics: `tts.ttfb`/`tts.total` measured in `_synth_ahead` (true synth latency), `e2e.ttfa` in the pacer (caller-perceived first audio). **(3)** `vieneu_vllm._probe_server_alive` now latches a process-level `_probed_ok` (success only) so per-clause WS reconnects to the resident service don't each re-probe vLLM (startup fail-fast preserved — a failed probe keeps raising). Branch `feat/seperate-service`. | First-audio was fast but playback stuttered at clause boundaries; the 40 ms playout buffer couldn't cover the per-clause synth gap, and synthesis didn't overlap playback. |
| 2026-06-29 | **Resident TTS service can run on vLLM (`TTS_SERVICE_ENGINE`).** New config `tts_service_engine` (`TTS_SERVICE_ENGINE`, default `vieneu`) selects what `voice/service/tts_server.py` uses INTERNALLY: `vieneu` (in-process transformers backbone + token streaming, GPU here) or `vieneu_vllm` (client to a separate vLLM server — GPU backbone in vLLM, only the ONNX codec runs in the service). `tts_server` now builds via `_make_tts()` (lazy-imports the chosen `Async*` class — both share the same stream/set_voice/pause/list_voices interface, drop-in) and warms the selected engine at startup (`vieneu_vllm` warm also probes vLLM `/health` → fail-fast at boot if the server is down). The **worker is unchanged** (`STT_BACKEND=remote TTS_BACKEND=remote`); switching the service's engine is a one-env-var change with no worker/topology change. Verified end-to-end over WS: warm TTFB ~300 ms through the full worker→service→vLLM→codec chain. Existing `test_tts_server.py` retargeted from the removed top-level `AsyncVieNeuTTS` import to `_make_tts`. Branch `feat/seperate-service`. | User wanted the vLLM speedup while keeping the familiar resident-service architecture (run the TTS service, worker stays `remote`) — not a new worker topology. |
| 2026-06-29 | **TTS backend `vieneu_vllm` — santa-tts served by a separate vLLM server (client).** New `voice/tts/vieneu_vllm.py`: a CLIENT backend (`VieNeuVLLMTTS`/`AsyncVieNeuVLLMTTS`) that streams `<|speech_N|>` tokens from a standalone vLLM OpenAI server and feeds them into the **same Phase-1 codec SINK** (`stream_speech_tokens_to_audio`). The worker process does NOT load the GPU backbone — vLLM owns it; the client loads only the ONNX CPU codec (`_load_codec_only` builds `VieNeuTTS(backbone_repo="", ...)` — `""` not `None`, the lib does `backbone_repo.rstrip()` before its load guard) + a standalone HF tokenizer + presets. Prompt = non-chat santa format by default, **chat-format when `VIENEU_USE_CHAT_FORMAT=true`** (mirrors the local path; else garbled audio only on vLLM). Spike-verified `/v1/completions` request: `prompt`=token ids, `stream`, `min_tokens` (else instant EOS), `stop_token_ids=[<|SPEECH_GENERATION_END|>]`, `skip_special_tokens=false`; one speech token per SSE piece → fragment==frame. `max_tokens` clamped to `max_model_len - len(prompt)` (else HTTP 400). Construction does a `/health` liveness probe → **raises if the server is down so the init-time fallback chain reaches local `vieneu`** (no mid-session fallback). Config `VIENEU_VLLM_{URL,MODEL,TEMPERATURE,TOP_K,MAX_TOKENS=1024,MIN_TOKENS=50,MAX_MODEL_LEN=2048}`; `TTS_FALLBACK_CHAIN` = `remote → vieneu_vllm → elevenlabs → vieneu`; launcher `scripts/run_vllm_tts.sh`. **vLLM runs on RTX 5060 Ti sm_120/CUDA13** but the host has NO CUDA toolkit/nvcc → server MUST run with `VLLM_USE_FLASHINFER_SAMPLER=0 VLLM_ATTENTION_BACKEND=FLASH_ATTN --enforce-eager` (else flashinfer JIT fails). **Measured (santa-tts, live server): TTFB P50 354 ms, total P50 ~3.1 s** (vs local-streaming 444 ms / 4744 ms — vLLM ~5.8 ms/tok vs 9.5, so it also fixes Phase-1's total regression). Docker GPU passthrough unavailable on this host (no nvidia-container-toolkit) → server runs from a separate venv, not the main `.venv`. Branch `feat/seperate-service`. | vLLM's serving engine cuts per-token latency AND total; separate-server topology keeps its torch off the main stack and matches the resident-service split. |
| 2026-06-29 | **TTS vieneu santa: token-level streaming (TTFA win).** New `NOBLE_TTS_STREAMING` (default `true`) in `config.py`; `voice/tts/vieneu.py` `VieNeuTTS.stream()` branches on it. The safetensors path's `infer_stream` was non-streaming (generate whole sentence → decode once → TTFB==total ≈3.9s). New design splits **token SOURCE ↔ codec SINK**: `stream_speech_tokens_to_audio(tts, voice, token_text_iter, *, stop_event)` is a source-agnostic incremental decoder mirroring the lib's GGUF-only `_infer_stream_ggml` chunk math (uses `tts._decode`/`_apply_watermark`/`streaming_*` only, never generation); the Phase-1 SOURCE `_local_token_source` runs `backbone.generate` on a child thread with a **token-ID `BaseStreamer`** (NOT `TextIteratorStreamer` — it buffers space-less `<|speech_N|>` until the end, killing the stream; the subclass skips the prompt and emits exactly one `<|speech_N|>` per token id so fragment==frame). Barge-in: `async pause()` on `VieNeuTTS`/`AsyncVieNeuTTS` sets a per-turn `_stop_event` (under a lock) checked in the streamer + sink; `tts_server.py` sets it on WS-close. **Measured (santa-tts, RTX 5060 Ti, ~150-char): TTFB P50 3888→444 ms (~8.8×); total 3889→4744 ms (+22%, per-chunk decode/overlap overhead, still < real-time playback so RTF<1).** Flag OFF = exact one-shot rollback. Branch `feat/seperate-service`. | Caller perceives first-audio, not total. The autoregressive generate is launch-bound (bf16/compile only bought ~10%); overlapping generation with playback is the real perceived-latency win. (Phase 2 — vLLM serving engine as a separate server feeding the same SINK — is planned but gated behind a sm_120/cu130 feasibility spike.) |
| 2026-06-29 | **TTS vieneu santa backbone: bf16 cast + optional torch.compile.** New `_accelerate_backbone(tts)` in `voice/tts/vieneu.py` (called from `_load_tts_santa`): casts the safetensors backbone to `VIENEU_BACKBONE_DTYPE` (default `bfloat16` — transformers `from_pretrained` loads fp32, idling Blackwell tensor cores) and, if `VIENEU_COMPILE_BACKBONE=true` (default `false`, distinct env from the library's own `VIENEU_COMPILE` to avoid wrong-order compile), `torch.compile(mode="reduce-overhead")` AFTER the cast, then re-warms. No-op for gguf/cpu backbones. **Measured (santa-tts, RTX 5060 Ti, ~150-char sentence, TTFB==total since this path is non-streaming):** baseline fp32 P50 **4405 ms** → bf16 **4088 ms** (~7%) → bf16+compile **3971 ms** (~10%). Latency split: generate **~3577 ms / ~90%** (387 tok @ 9.5 ms/tok), CPU ONNX decode ~243 ms. Conclusion: the path is launch/Python-overhead bound on HF `generate()` (compile graph-breaks → ~no gain), NOT compute-bound — bf16 kept on as a free win, compile left off-by-default. Real levers remain a faster serving engine (vLLM/LMDeploy) and token-level streaming (the safetensors `infer_stream` generates the whole sentence before the first chunk). Branch `feat/seperate-service`. | User reported slow TTS latency despite GPU under-utilization + free VRAM; investigation pinned the cost to the autoregressive generate loop, not compute. |

| 2026-06-09 | Added opt-in `qdrant` RAG backend (dense via remote Qdrant + local BM25 RRF). New `agent/rag/qdrant_store.py`, `qdrant_adapter.py`, `FlatStore.load_chunks_only()`, `scripts/build_qdrant.py`; `rag-qdrant` extra; `needs_qdrant` marker; `qdrant_collection` default → `noble_kb_e5_1024d`. Fixed `/api/config` `rag.provider` to reflect the backend + health probe gated on `qdrant`; removed dead `flat_store_adapter` MemoryRouter adapters (imported a deleted `vector_store`). | User wanted the vector store centralized on the EC2 Qdrant — shared across instances, updatable without redeploy — instead of gitignored local `.npy`/BM25 artifacts. |
| 2026-06-04 | Thinking **display routing**: `NOBLE_LLM_SHOW_THINKING` (default off). Facade `_Callbacks` takes a display `ThinkStripper` — `stream_response(show_thinking=)` strips `<think>` from `on_token` unless shown; `text_chat` passes `settings.llm_show_thinking`, voice never does (TTS always strips). `eval/predict.py` `strip_think`s predictions + builds its client with `enable_thinking`. History channel (client return) untouched → thinking persists cross-turn when generation is on. Client unchanged. | User wanted, with thinking ON, the model to keep full reasoning in history (forwarded to LLM) while hiding it from chat UI (debug-toggleable), TTS, and eval. |
| 2026-06-04 | Global thinking toggle `NOBLE_LLM_ENABLE_THINKING` (default off). New `agent/llm/thinking.py` (`disable_thinking_kwargs`, `ThinkStripper`, `strip_think`); `StreamingClient(enable_thinking=...)` injects the per-provider disable kwarg and strips leaked `<think>...</think>` from stream + `complete_once`. Wired via `facade` from `settings.llm_enable_thinking`. | Running Qwen3-0.6B on vLLM leaked `<think>` reasoning into responses/TTS/history; user wanted one switch to disable thinking across all 3 providers even on thinking-capable models. |
| 2026-06-04 | Per-path **primary-provider pin**: `NOBLE_AGENT_MODEL` / `NOBLE_SUMMARY_MODEL` / `NOBLE_SEMCACHE_MODEL` each look up the provider whose `<P>_MODEL` equals the value (`config.primary_for`). `StreamingClient` accepts a `primary=` kwarg and moves that provider to the front of the chain (fallback unchanged). `NOBLE_SUMMARY_K` / `NOBLE_SUMMARY_KEEP_TAIL` counted in user-turns. Dropped the legacy `OPENAI_MODEL` env-override mutation of `agent_def.model`. | User wanted each path (main agent, summary, semcache) to explicitly designate WHICH of the configured providers it runs on — not rely on "first provider that accepts the name" routing — while keeping fallback for resilience. |
| 2026-06-04 | Rewrote CLAUDE.md for unified single-agent (`noble_agent`); removed legacy / refactor sections; added Self-Maintenance Rules. | User adopted unified as the primary codebase; legacy + refactor packages deleted. |
| 2026-06-03 | Unified single-agent design landed in `src/noble_agent/`; multi-agent router/consultant/support removed; semcache moved behind a flag. | See spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-06-03-noble-agent-unified-design.md`. |
